It has been like this constantly for the last year and eight months.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there was a specific period, even brief, within the last year and eight months where your symptoms met the full criteria for a major depressive episode, lasting at least two weeks, or has it been a consistent, lower-grade depression throughout?\n\nPatient: I'm not quite sure about that, and I haven't really thought about it in those terms.     "content": "Previous Candidates:\n[\"Major Depressive Disorder\", \"Persistent Depressive Disorder\"]\n\nInterview Transcript:\n\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see us today?\n\nPatient: I'm here because I've been struggling with a really low, depressed mood for a long time now. It's been about a year and eight months of just feeling constantly sad, and I haven't been able to shake it.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about your low mood over the past year and eight months, has it been consistently severe or have you had periods where your mood improved slightly?\n\nPatient: It has been completely constant, with no breaks or times where I felt any better. Every single day for the past year and eight months has felt just as heavy and gloomy. I really haven't had any periods where my mood improved, even slightly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest or pleasure in activities that you normally enjoy, or has that remained relatively unchanged throughout this period?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've completely lost interest in all the hobbies and activities I used to enjoy. Even when I try to force myself to do them, I don't feel any happiness or pleasure at all. It just feels like those positive feelings have completely vanished.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your low mood and loss of interest in activities as being moderately depressing or severely debilitating, interfering significantly with your daily life and relationships?\n\nPatient: It is definitely severely debilitating and has completely disrupted my life. I've lost all motivation to do daily tasks or work, and I've completely withdrawn from my friends and family because I can't keep up with relationships anymore. It has been like this constantly for the last year and eight months.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there was a specific period, even brief, within the last year and eight months where your symptoms met the full criteria for a major depressive episode, lasting at least two weeks, or has it been a consistent, lower-grade depression throughout?\n\nPatient: I'm not quite sure about that, and I haven't really thought about it in those terms. To me, these feelings have just felt generally continuous and ongoing for the entire year and eight months.\n\nDoctor: Let me try to clarify: during this year and eight months, have you had any distinct periods where you felt intensely sad, hopeless, or worthless, or where you had significant changes in sleep, appetite, or energy, that might have stood out as particularly bad times compared to the rest?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure about any changes in my sleep, appetite, or energy. I haven't noticed any distinct periods where things got worse, because this sadness and gloomy mood have just been mostly continuous for the entire year and eight months.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back over the past year and eight months and tell me if there was ever a two-week period where you experienced at least five symptoms of depression, such as depressed mood, loss of interest, changes in appetite or sleep, fatigue, feelings of worthlessness, or difficulty concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've actually experienced those symptoms almost continuously for the entire year and eight months, rather than just a two-week stretch. I constantly feel a deeply depressed mood, a total loss of interest in things, and severe difficulty concentrating. On top of that, I just feel completely empty and sad all the time.\n\nDoctor: Given that you've experienced a range of depressive symptoms continuously for over a year, can you help me understand if there have been any periods, even if brief, where these symptoms may have intensified or reached a peak, potentially meeting the criteria for a major depressive episode within the context of your longer-term depressive experience?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, as my symptoms have felt consistently flat and heavy throughout the entire year and eight months. There haven't been any distinct periods where things suddenly intensified or peaked further. It has just been one long, continuous stretch of feeling exactly like this.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your symptoms, although consistently severe, may have had periods where they were slightly more or less severe, even if it didn't feel like a significant peak or improvement to you?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure about that. I haven't noticed any periods where the symptoms were even slightly better or worse, as they have just felt consistently present the entire time.\n\nDoctor: Considering your symptoms have been consistently present and severe for over a year, do you think your experience is more accurately described as a long-term, low-grade depression or as a condition with periods of more intense depressive episodes, even if those periods are difficult to distinguish from the overall pattern of your symptoms?\n\nPatient: It is really difficult to distinguish and I am not quite sure, but it has just felt like a heavy, constant cloud of sadness and numbness for the entire year and a half. There haven't been any clear ups and downs or changes that would help me tell them apart."
